Well, um... I wouldn't say my personality changed. I'm still the same guy, still a team player. But... I guess my routine has changed a bit. I hope you don't think less of me, but I'm actually not working the ambulance anymore right now. 

They, um... they had moved me to desk duty for a little while, just doing run notes and equipment checks. But because I was trying so hard to make sure every single detail was perfect, I guess I got a little behind. They said I was being too unreliable with it, so... they actually took me off the schedule completely. Just for now, though! I'm sure they just want me to rest and get my bearings. That's a pretty standard thing for a department to do, right? To give you time to recharge? 

And, well, since then, I guess I've mostly stopped seeing my friends. I haven't really been keeping up with things around the house the way I used to, either. I've just been staying in. But I really am trying to get everything back on track, I promise. It's normal to need a little time to yourself to figure things out when work gets complicated, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.

Well, um... I guess you could say that. I mean, I wouldn't call it anxiety, exactly. I'm not a nervous person! I'm an EMT, so I'm used to high-pressure situations. I hope you don't think I'm just getting flustered over nothing. 

It's more like... a sense of responsibility? Like, if I don't do it, things just won't be right. Unwanted? Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm... like I'm losing control or anything like that.

It felt like... like the whole bay would go bad if I broke the sequence. I was just trying to keep everything safe. Does that make sense? I really didn't mean to cause any trouble.\n\nDoctor: When you were arranging the bags and repeating the numbers, did you feel like you had to do it to get rid of a thought or fear that kept bothering you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, it wasn't... I wasn't trying to get rid of a bad thought or anything like that. I wasn't scared. I really don't want you to think I was panicking or... or bothered by something in my head. \n\nIt was just... I just wanted to make sure it was perfect. In my line of work, being prepared is just really important, you know? So I was just being thorough with the sequence. I hope that doesn't sound too strange. I really wasn't trying to be difficult or cause a scene, I promise. I just wanted to do a good job... that's a normal thing to want, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of strong need for order or perfection for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I've always liked things to be organized, sure. But not... not like this. It hasn't always been this strong. \n\nI guess it started getting a little more intense lately, but I really think it's just because work has been so busy, you know? Anyone would want to be extra careful when things get hectic. I just want to make sure I'm doing my part right. That's a good thing, isn't it? Being dedicated to the job? I really don't think it's anything to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Did this change come on suddenly after a specific event or medical problem?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't call it a problem, exactly. But, I guess... well, about eight months ago, I was on a transfer. We were moving a patient, and I ended up hurting my shoulder and my ribs pretty bad. \n\nIt was just an accident, you know? A normal hazard of the job. But I guess... maybe after that, I started wanting to be a little more careful with how things were set up. Just to make sure we were ready for anything, so nobody else would get hurt. That makes sense, right? It's just me trying to be a responsible EMT... I hope you can see it that way.\n\nDoctor: After that injury, did you notice any change in your personality or behavior compared with before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say my personality changed. I'm still the same guy, still a team player. But... I guess my routine has changed a bit. I hope you don't think less of me, but I'm actually not working the ambulance anymore right now. \n\nThey, um... they had moved me to desk duty for a little while, just doing run notes and equipment checks. But because I was trying so hard to make sure every single detail was perfect, I guess I got a little behind. They said I was being too unreliable with it, so... they actually took me off the schedule completely. Just for now, though! I'm sure they just want me to rest and get my bearings. That's a pretty standard thing for a department to do, right? To give you time to recharge? \n\nAnd, well, since then, I guess I've mostly stopped seeing my friends. I haven't really been keeping up with things around the house the way I used to, either. I've just been staying in. But I really am trying to get everything back on track, I promise. It's normal to need a little time to yourself to figure things out when work gets complicated, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any head injury or concussion around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, nothing like that. I didn't hit my head or anything when we had the accident. Just the shoulder and the ribs, like I mentioned. \n\nI was actually really careful to tuck my chin and protect my head—we're trained to do that, you know? That's a good thing, right? Because head injuries can be so serious, and I definitely wouldn't want to complicate things for the department, or... or for you, while you're trying to help me figure this out. So, no concussions at all. My head is totally fine. I hope that's the right answer, or at least helps rule some things out for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to stop these repeating thoughts or actions, even when you know they’re unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... sometimes it is hard to stop. I really don't want you to think I'm being difficult or stubborn or anything like that. I just... I want to do a good job, you know? \n\nBut, um... well, part of the reason they sent me here was because of something that happened at the station. I was... I was just trying to organize the airway bags. I had this specific pattern, and I knew—I just knew—it was the right way to do it. And I was sort of... repeating the dispatch numbers out loud while I was doing it. Just to keep track, you know? It helps me focus. \n\nBut then my lieutenant told me to stop and step away, and... I couldn't. I really couldn't. I wasn't trying to be insubordinate, I swear. It just felt like... if I broke the sequence, if I didn't finish it exactly right, the whole bay was going to go bad. Like something terrible would happen. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly when I say it out loud like that... unreasonable, like you said. I mean, like I was saying, I haven't been seeing my friends as much lately anyway, so I guess we haven't really had the chance to have deep conversations about it. But... no, nobody has pulled me aside and told me I'm acting like a different person or anything. 

I always try to be polite and easy to get along with, you know? I'd feel terrible if I was making people uncomfortable or acting strange around them. If I had really changed for the worse, I'm sure someone would have told me by now, right? Since they haven't said anything, I must still be coming across okay... don't you think?

Um... perfectionistic? Well, I mean... I've always cared about doing a good job, you know? Especially being an EMT, you kind of have to make sure you get the details right so nobody gets hurt.

I hope that's an okay answer? I just really haven't noticed anything like what you're describing, and I definitely don't feel upset like that. I'm doing okay in that department, I think... right?

I just wanted to make sure it was perfect. In my line of work, being prepared is just really important, you know? So I was just being thorough with the sequence. I hope that doesn't sound too strange. I really wasn't trying to be difficult or cause a scene, I promise. I just wanted to do a good job... that's a normal thing to want, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had this kind of strong need for order or perfection for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I've always liked things to be organized, sure. But not... not like this. It hasn't always been this strong. \n\nI guess it started getting a little more intense lately, but I really think it's just because work has been so busy, you know? Anyone would want to be extra careful when things get hectic. I just want to make sure I'm doing my part right. That's a good thing, isn't it? Being dedicated to the job? I really don't think it's anything to worry about.\n\nDoctor: Did this change come on suddenly after a specific event or medical problem?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't call it a problem, exactly. But, I guess... well, about eight months ago, I was on a transfer. We were moving a patient, and I ended up hurting my shoulder and my ribs pretty bad. \n\nIt was just an accident, you know? A normal hazard of the job. But I guess... maybe after that, I started wanting to be a little more careful with how things were set up. Just to make sure we were ready for anything, so nobody else would get hurt. That makes sense, right? It's just me trying to be a responsible EMT... I hope you can see it that way.\n\nDoctor: After that injury, did you notice any change in your personality or behavior compared with before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say my personality changed. I'm still the same guy, still a team player. But... I guess my routine has changed a bit. I hope you don't think less of me, but I'm actually not working the ambulance anymore right now. \n\nThey, um... they had moved me to desk duty for a little while, just doing run notes and equipment checks. But because I was trying so hard to make sure every single detail was perfect, I guess I got a little behind. They said I was being too unreliable with it, so... they actually took me off the schedule completely. Just for now, though! I'm sure they just want me to rest and get my bearings. That's a pretty standard thing for a department to do, right? To give you time to recharge? \n\nAnd, well, since then, I guess I've mostly stopped seeing my friends. I haven't really been keeping up with things around the house the way I used to, either. I've just been staying in. But I really am trying to get everything back on track, I promise. It's normal to need a little time to yourself to figure things out when work gets complicated, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any head injury or concussion around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, nothing like that. I didn't hit my head or anything when we had the accident. Just the shoulder and the ribs, like I mentioned. \n\nI was actually really careful to tuck my chin and protect my head—we're trained to do that, you know? That's a good thing, right? Because head injuries can be so serious, and I definitely wouldn't want to complicate things for the department, or... or for you, while you're trying to help me figure this out. So, no concussions at all. My head is totally fine. I hope that's the right answer, or at least helps rule some things out for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to stop these repeating thoughts or actions, even when you know they’re unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... sometimes it is hard to stop. I really don't want you to think I'm being difficult or stubborn or anything like that. I just... I want to do a good job, you know? \n\nBut, um... well, part of the reason they sent me here was because of something that happened at the station. I was... I was just trying to organize the airway bags. I had this specific pattern, and I knew—I just knew—it was the right way to do it. And I was sort of... repeating the dispatch numbers out loud while I was doing it. Just to keep track, you know? It helps me focus. \n\nBut then my lieutenant told me to stop and step away, and... I couldn't. I really couldn't. I wasn't trying to be insubordinate, I swear. It just felt like... if I broke the sequence, if I didn't finish it exactly right, the whole bay was going to go bad. Like something terrible would happen. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little silly when I say it out loud like that... unreasonable, like you said. But in the moment, it just felt so important to keep everyone safe. I hope you understand that my intentions were good. I just want to be a helpful part of the crew again. That's a good thing to want, right?\n\nDoctor: When you’re doing these organizing or repeating rituals, do you feel driven to do them to relieve anxiety or discomfort?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess you could say that. I mean, I wouldn't call it anxiety, exactly. I'm not a nervous person! I'm an EMT, so I'm used to high-pressure situations. I hope you don't think I'm just getting flustered over nothing. \n\nIt's more like... a sense of responsibility? Like, if I don't do it, things just won't be right. And when I do get the sequence perfect, or the bags organized exactly how they need to be, it just feels... better. Like I've done my part to keep everyone safe. It's just me trying to be thorough, you know? Making sure everything is perfectly in order so we're ready for the next call. \n\nI guess it does make me feel a little more settled once it's done. But that's a normal way to feel when you finish a task properly, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way. That makes sense, right?\n\nDoctor: When you get the urge to arrange things or repeat numbers, does it feel unwanted or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: Unwanted? Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm... like I'm losing control or anything like that. \n\nIt's actually the opposite, really. It feels like something I *need* to do, to be helpful. It doesn't feel like some strange thought invading my head, if that's what you mean. It just feels like... like an important idea that I should follow through on. Because I want to do a good job, you know? \n\nI really don't mind doing it. I mean, sure, maybe my lieutenant didn't understand it, but to me, it just feels like I'm being extra careful. And being careful is a good trait for an EMT to have, right? I'm just trying to be the best I can be for the team. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these behaviors are actually under your control, or do they seem like they’re just how you are now compared with before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... I mean, I don't really think it's a matter of losing control. I really hope you don't think I'm just out of control or, you know, changing into a different person or something. \n\nI'm still me! I'm still the same guy who just wants to help people. I think... maybe I'm just paying a little more attention to the details now than I used to. Like I said, I just want to make sure everything is perfectly safe. \n\nSo, I mean, I *could* control it, I suppose, if I really, really had to. It's just that... when I'm doing it, it feels so necessary. It feels like the right thing to do. So I choose to do it, because I want to be thorough. That doesn't sound like I'm out of control, does it? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm... well, you know, completely losing my grip on things. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you’ve become a different person since this started?\n\nPatient: A different person? Oh, no... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I hope it doesn't seem that way to you. \n\nI'm still just me, you know? The same guy who loves his job, the same guy who just wants to help people out there on the rig. I'd hate to think anyone looks at me and sees someone... well, someone broken or weird. Maybe I'm just going through a little stretch where I'm more focused on getting all the details perfect, but deep down, I'm exactly the same. \n\nI haven't changed into someone bad or unstable or anything like that. I'm still a good EMT. You can see that, right? I really hope I'm still coming across as myself.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you noticed any change in how much you care about other people’s feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I wouldn't say I don't care about people's feelings. I still care! I'm a really caring person, I promise. It's just... \n\nI guess lately, I haven't been reaching out as much. Like, most days I just sort of sit in my apartment. I have these... these trees in the corner, and I just sit there. I don't even really cook anymore, even though the food is just sitting right there in the fridge. And I haven't been calling the guys for basketball like I used to. \n\nBut it's not because I don't care about them! I hope it doesn't sound like I'm turning cold or mean or anything. It's just... it's really hard to get myself to start anything right now. It's like I just don't have the energy to feel much of anything, you know? But I still want to be a good friend. I'm just taking a little break to recharge, I think. That makes sense, doesn't it? I'm not a bad person for needing some quiet time, right?\n\nDoctor: Since the injury, have other people who know you well said that your personality has changed?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not that I can think of. Nobody has really said anything like that to me. \n\nI mean, like I was saying, I haven't been seeing my friends as much lately anyway, so I guess we haven't really had the chance to have deep conversations about it. But... no, nobody has pulled me aside and told me I'm acting like a different person or anything. \n\nI always try to be polite and easy to get along with, you know? I'd feel terrible if I was making people uncomfortable or acting strange around them. If I had really changed for the worse, I'm sure someone would have told me by now, right? Since they haven't said anything, I must still be coming across okay... don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Before the injury, were you already this perfectionistic and rigid about rules and order?\n\nPatient: Um... perfectionistic? Well, I mean... I've always cared about doing a good job, you know? Especially being an EMT, you kind of have to make sure you get the details right so nobody gets hurt. \n\nBut I wouldn't say I was... rigid. That makes it sound like I'm stubborn or hard to work with, and I really try to be a good team player. I promise I'm not difficult about things. I just... I've always liked to follow the protocols because they're there to keep people safe. \n\nThat's just being responsible, isn't it? I really hope it doesn't come across like I'm obsessed with rules in a bad way or anything. I just want to do things the right way so I can be helpful. Being careful is a good thing for a medic to be, right?\n\nDoctor: Since the injury, have you noticed any lasting change in your personality, like being more impulsive, irritable, or unlike your usual self?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I really try hard to keep my cool, you know? Especially in my line of work, you can't just be impulsive or lose your temper, so I've always made it a point to be patient with people. I haven't been snapping at anyone or acting out, I promise. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm a little quieter these days, like I said before, but I'm definitely not angry or... or irritable. I'd hate to make anyone feel bad or be difficult to be around. I hope I've been pleasant with you today? I really always try to put my best foot forward and just be easy to talk to. That's how I come across, isn't it? I really don't think I've changed in a bad way like that.\n\nDoctor: When you feel the urge to do these rituals, do they feel distressing or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Um... rituals? I... I don't think I really have any rituals, to be honest. I wouldn't want to do anything that seems strange or gets in the way of my day, you know? I promise I'm not doing anything weird or acting out like that. I just try to stay focused and do what I'm supposed to do so I don't cause any problems for anyone. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just really haven't noticed anything like what you're describing, and I definitely don't feel upset like that. Um... no, definitely not. I haven't had any kind of brain event or... or serious medical illness. I'm actually really healthy. You kind of have to be to work on an ambulance, you know? We do a lot of heavy lifting and moving fast, so I make sure to stay in good shape.

That's a good thing to want, right?\n\nDoctor: When you’re doing these organizing or repeating rituals, do you feel driven to do them to relieve anxiety or discomfort?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess you could say that. I mean, I wouldn't call it anxiety, exactly. I'm not a nervous person! I'm an EMT, so I'm used to high-pressure situations. I hope you don't think I'm just getting flustered over nothing. \n\nIt's more like... a sense of responsibility? Like, if I don't do it, things just won't be right. And when I do get the sequence perfect, or the bags organized exactly how they need to be, it just feels... better. Like I've done my part to keep everyone safe. It's just me trying to be thorough, you know? Making sure everything is perfectly in order so we're ready for the next call. \n\nI guess it does make me feel a little more settled once it's done. But that's a normal way to feel when you finish a task properly, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing things the right way. That makes sense, right?\n\nDoctor: When you get the urge to arrange things or repeat numbers, does it feel unwanted or intrusive to you?\n\nPatient: Unwanted? Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say that. I mean, I hope it doesn't sound like I'm... like I'm losing control or anything like that. \n\nIt's actually the opposite, really. It feels like something I *need* to do, to be helpful. It doesn't feel like some strange thought invading my head, if that's what you mean. It just feels like... like an important idea that I should follow through on. Because I want to do a good job, you know? \n\nI really don't mind doing it. I mean, sure, maybe my lieutenant didn't understand it, but to me, it just feels like I'm being extra careful. And being careful is a good trait for an EMT to have, right? I'm just trying to be the best I can be for the team. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel these behaviors are actually under your control, or do they seem like they’re just how you are now compared with before?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... I mean, I don't really think it's a matter of losing control. I really hope you don't think I'm just out of control or, you know, changing into a different person or something. \n\nI'm still me! I'm still the same guy who just wants to help people. I think... maybe I'm just paying a little more attention to the details now than I used to. Like I said, I just want to make sure everything is perfectly safe. \n\nSo, I mean, I *could* control it, I suppose, if I really, really had to. It's just that... when I'm doing it, it feels so necessary. It feels like the right thing to do. So I choose to do it, because I want to be thorough. That doesn't sound like I'm out of control, does it? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you don't think I'm... well, you know, completely losing my grip on things. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you’ve become a different person since this started?\n\nPatient: A different person? Oh, no... no, I really don't think so. I mean, I hope it doesn't seem that way to you. \n\nI'm still just me, you know? The same guy who loves his job, the same guy who just wants to help people out there on the rig. I'd hate to think anyone looks at me and sees someone... well, someone broken or weird. Maybe I'm just going through a little stretch where I'm more focused on getting all the details perfect, but deep down, I'm exactly the same. \n\nI haven't changed into someone bad or unstable or anything like that. I'm still a good EMT. You can see that, right? I really hope I'm still coming across as myself.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you noticed any change in how much you care about other people’s feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I mean, I wouldn't say I don't care about people's feelings. I still care! I'm a really caring person, I promise. It's just... \n\nI guess lately, I haven't been reaching out as much. Like, most days I just sort of sit in my apartment. I have these... these trees in the corner, and I just sit there. I don't even really cook anymore, even though the food is just sitting right there in the fridge. And I haven't been calling the guys for basketball like I used to. \n\nBut it's not because I don't care about them! I hope it doesn't sound like I'm turning cold or mean or anything. It's just... it's really hard to get myself to start anything right now. It's like I just don't have the energy to feel much of anything, you know? But I still want to be a good friend. I'm just taking a little break to recharge, I think. That makes sense, doesn't it? I'm not a bad person for needing some quiet time, right?\n\nDoctor: Since the injury, have other people who know you well said that your personality has changed?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not that I can think of. Nobody has really said anything like that to me. \n\nI mean, like I was saying, I haven't been seeing my friends as much lately anyway, so I guess we haven't really had the chance to have deep conversations about it. But... no, nobody has pulled me aside and told me I'm acting like a different person or anything. \n\nI always try to be polite and easy to get along with, you know? I'd feel terrible if I was making people uncomfortable or acting strange around them. If I had really changed for the worse, I'm sure someone would have told me by now, right? Since they haven't said anything, I must still be coming across okay... don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Before the injury, were you already this perfectionistic and rigid about rules and order?\n\nPatient: Um... perfectionistic? Well, I mean... I've always cared about doing a good job, you know? Especially being an EMT, you kind of have to make sure you get the details right so nobody gets hurt. \n\nBut I wouldn't say I was... rigid. Being careful is a good thing for a medic to be, right?\n\nDoctor: Since the injury, have you noticed any lasting change in your personality, like being more impulsive, irritable, or unlike your usual self?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I really try hard to keep my cool, you know? Especially in my line of work, you can't just be impulsive or lose your temper, so I've always made it a point to be patient with people. I haven't been snapping at anyone or acting out, I promise. \n\nI mean, maybe I'm a little quieter these days, like I said before, but I'm definitely not angry or... or irritable. I'd hate to make anyone feel bad or be difficult to be around. I hope I've been pleasant with you today? I really always try to put my best foot forward and just be easy to talk to. That's how I come across, isn't it? I really don't think I've changed in a bad way like that.\n\nDoctor: When you feel the urge to do these rituals, do they feel distressing or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Um... rituals? I... I don't think I really have any rituals, to be honest. I mean, I have normal routines, just like anyone else, but nothing that feels... upsetting or distressing. \n\nI really try to just keep things normal and manageable. I wouldn't want to do anything that seems strange or gets in the way of my day, you know? I promise I'm not doing anything weird or acting out like that. I just try to stay focused and do what I'm supposed to do so I don't cause any problems for anyone. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just really haven't noticed anything like what you're describing, and I definitely don't feel upset like that. I'm doing okay in that department, I think... right?\n\nDoctor: Do you think these organizing and repeating behaviors are excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Um... excessive? No, I don't think so. That makes it sound like I'm out of control or doing something wrong, and I'm really not. Everyone likes a clean space, don't they? I hope that doesn't make me sound unreasonable... I'm just trying to do things the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you get repeated thoughts or images that you don’t want and can’t get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Um... repeated thoughts or images? I'm pretty good at just shaking things off and focusing on what's in front of me. In my job, you have to be able to just move on from things, so I've gotten really good at keeping a clear head. \n\nI promise I don't have any crazy thoughts bothering me or anything like that. I just try to keep things positive and stay focused on doing a good job. I hope that's what you're looking for? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here... I just don't have anything like that going on, I swear.\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors feel unwanted or against what you want to do?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not unwanted. I mean, why would I do something I didn't want to do? That wouldn't make much sense, right? \n\nI actually want to keep things organized. Like I was saying, it's just... it's just me being careful and proactive. When you work on an ambulance, you have to know exactly where every piece of equipment is the second you need it, so keeping things neat is just a choice I make. It's a good habit to have, don't you think? \n\nI promise I'm in total control of what I'm doing. It's not like I'm being forced to do anything against my will, or doing things I don't want to do. I just want everything to be right so I don't let anyone down or mess up. I'm just trying to be responsible. Does that sound okay to you? I really hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression here... I'm just doing what I think is best.\n\nDoctor: Did the behavior change begin after any serious medical illness, brain event, or other neurological problem?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I haven't had any kind of brain event or... or serious medical illness. I'm actually really healthy. You kind of have to be to work on an ambulance, you know? We do a lot of heavy lifting and moving fast, so I make sure to stay in good shape. \n\nI haven't had any head injuries or neurological problems at all. I take really good care of myself, I promise. I wouldn't want you to think there's something physically wrong with my brain or anything scary like that. I'm just a normal, healthy guy doing my best. That's a good thing, right? I hope that clears that up... I really don't have any medical issues like that, I swear."
